1.  Overview &amp; Goals
The Algoleap Deal / Opportunity Management System (DMS) is the natural next step after the Lead Management System. Where the LMS captures and qualifies opportunities, the DMS takes over once a lead is formally qualified and moves it through a structured sales closing process.
The DMS is built on top of the existing LMS codebase, sharing the same React + Node.js + PostgreSQL stack, RBAC system, and UI shell. It introduces a Deal entity that is either converted from a qualified Lead or created directly (for deals that skip the lead stage).
1.1  Primary Goals of v1
Convert qualified Leads into Deals with one click, preserving all context.
Track Deals through a structured closing pipeline: Discovery â†’ Proposal â†’ Negotiation â†’ Contract â†’ Closed Won / Closed Lost.
Provide real-time pipeline visibility with revenue forecasting by stage.
Capture structured deal data: contract value, payment terms, decision timeline, competition.
Maintain a complete activity log of every deal touch â€” calls, emails, meetings, notes.
Generate a Deal Room: a shared workspace for documents, decisions, and stakeholder sign-off.
Trigger automated notifications at key deal milestones.
1.2  Non-Goals for v1
CPQ (Configure, Price, Quote) â€” complex quoting engines are out of scope.
E-signature integration (DocuSign / Adobe Sign) â€” planned for v2.
Revenue recognition or invoicing â€” handled by finance systems.
Customer-facing deal portals â€” internal tool only.
AI-assisted win probability predictions â€” v2 feature.
1.3  Relationship to the LMS
Think of the LMS as the funnel top (awareness â†’ qualification) and the DMS as the funnel bottom (proposal â†’ closed). A Lead that reaches QUALIFIED status is the entry point for a Deal.
The two systems share: user accounts, roles, RBAC, accounts, contacts, file storage, and email infrastructure. The DMS adds new DB tables, new API routes under /api/deals, and new frontend pages â€” nothing in the existing LMS is removed or broken.
2.  Scope â€” What&apos;s In, What&apos;s Out (v1)

3.  User Roles &amp; Permissions
The DMS inherits all five LMS roles. The permission matrix below adds new deal-specific atomic permissions alongside the existing lead permissions.
3.1  New Atomic Permissions

5.  Database Design (Schema Extension)
The DMS adds four new tables. All existing LMS tables remain unchanged. Foreign keys link back to users, accounts, contacts, and leads.

5.2  Prisma Schema Extension
Paste this schema into schema.prisma below the existing Lead model. Run &apos;npx prisma db push&apos; after saving.
6.  API Design â€” Endpoints &amp; Contracts
All routes are prefixed with /api. Auth middleware (JWT) and permission checks apply to every endpoint. Methods follow REST conventions.

7.2  New Folder Additions
7.3  Navigation Update
Add two new items to the sidebar navigation, after the existing Leads and Accounts links:
Pipeline  â†’ /pipeline  (show for deals.view_all or deals.view_own)
Forecast  â†’ /forecast  (show for deals.view_forecast only)
8.  Pipeline &amp; Forecasting Views
8.1  Kanban Pipeline Board
The PipelinePage renders one column per active deal stage (Discovery, Proposal, Negotiation, Contract). Each card shows:
Deal title and account name
Contract value and weighted value (value Ã— probability %)
Owner avatar and expected close date
Color-coded staleness indicator (no activity in &gt; 7 days = amber; &gt; 14 days = red)
Next Step text (editable inline on hover)
Drag-and-drop between columns triggers a PATCH /deals/:id/stage call with state machine validation. If the transition is invalid, the card snaps back and shows an inline error toast.
8.2  Forecast Dashboard
The ForecastPage shows an executive-level revenue view:
Stage funnel bar chart: unweighted vs weighted pipeline total per stage.
Close date heat map: deals expected to close per week for the next 12 weeks.
Leaderboard: top deals by contract value in each stage.
Filters: by service line, owner, geography, deal type (New / Expansion / Renewal).
Date range selector for the forecast window.
Use Recharts (already likely in the LMS) for charts. Keep the chart library consistent with any existing dashboard widgets.
8.3  Probability Weighting

9.  Deal Scoring &amp; Probability
In v1, deal probability is a manual field (0â€“100) set by the deal owner. The UI surfaces helpful nudges:
If the deal has no ECONOMIC_BUYER stakeholder, show a warning: &apos;No economic buyer identified â€” this deal may stall.&apos;
If the deal has not had any activity in 14 days, show a staleness alert on the deal card.
If expectedCloseDate is in the past and the deal is still open, flag it as overdue.
These nudges are computed client-side on the deal list and do not require server changes.
9.1  Manual Override Rule
When a user manually sets probability, their value takes precedence over the stage default. Store a boolean field manualProbability on the deal to track this. When the stage changes, reset manualProbability to false unless the user explicitly types in a new number during the same stage transition.

10.2  Activity Feed
The DealDetailPage shows a chronological feed mixing automated events (stage changes, assignments) with manual log entries (calls, meetings, notes). Each activity entry shows: actor avatar, action description, timestamp, and optional body text.
Activity types: STAGE_CHANGED, NOTE, CALL, MEETING, EMAIL, DOCUMENT_ADDED, ASSIGNED.
11.  Integration with LMS (Lead â†’ Deal Conversion)
11.1  Conversion Flow
A Lead in QUALIFIED stage shows a &apos;Convert to Deal&apos; button visible to users with deals.create permission.
Clicking it opens the ConvertLeadModal, which:
Pre-fills all deal fields from the lead: title, account, contact, service line, estimated revenue, expected start date.
Lets the user confirm or adjust any pre-filled field before submitting.
On submit, calls POST /leads/:id/convert which: creates the Deal record, sets sourceLeadId, moves the Lead to a new terminal stage CONVERTED, and logs a CONVERTED activity on the lead.
Redirects the user to the new DealDetailPage.
11.2  Lead Stage Extension
Add CONVERTED as a new terminal stage in the Lead state machine. This is additive â€” no existing transitions change. A CONVERTED lead shows a prominent &apos;View Deal&apos; button that links to the created deal.

12.  File Attachments &amp; Document Management
The DMS extends the existing Supabase file storage with a dedicated Deal Room for each deal. Deal Room documents are categorized and versioned, unlike the simpler lead attachments.

12.2  Versioning
Uploading a document with the same fileName and category creates a new version (version field increments). The UI shows version history with a download link for each version. Only the latest version appears by default; older versions are accessible via &apos;Show history&apos;.
12.3  Storage Path
Files are stored in the existing Supabase &apos;lead-attachments&apos; bucket (or a new &apos;deal-documents&apos; bucket if preferred) at the path: deals/{dealId}/{category}/{timestamp}-{fileName}
13.  Security &amp; Permissions
All LMS security rules apply. The DMS adds:
Every /deals/* route must call permit() middleware with the appropriate deals.* permission key.
deals.view_own: the backend WHERE clause adds AND (deal.ownerId = req.user.id OR deal.practiceLeaderId = req.user.id).
deals.close_won requires a separate permission from deals.move_stage â€” closing a deal is a higher-stakes action.
CSV export strips any fields the requesting user is not permitted to view (e.g., contractValue hidden from Team Members).
Deal Room documents follow the same MIME type whitelist as lead attachments.
Soft-delete only: DELETE /deals/:id sets a deletedAt timestamp, not a physical row removal.
14.  Build Order â€” Week-by-Week Plan
Week 1 â€” Foundation
Extend Prisma schema with all new tables; push to Supabase.
Write seed data extensions (permissions, role defaults for deal permissions).
Build POST /deals and GET /deals API endpoints.
Add the &apos;Pipeline&apos; nav item; render a static placeholder page.
Week 2 â€” Core Deal CRUD
DealCreatePage: form with all required fields.
DealDetailPage: read-only view of all deal data.
DealEditPage: edit form with permission guard.
Lead â†’ Deal conversion: POST /leads/:id/convert + ConvertLeadModal.
Week 3 â€” Pipeline Kanban
PipelinePage: kanban with drag-and-drop.
POST /deals/:id/stage wired to the state machine.
Deal card staleness indicators.
Inline &apos;Next Step&apos; editing on cards.
Week 4 â€” Activity &amp; Deal Room
DealActivityFeed: log and display activities.
DealRoomPanel: upload, categorize, version documents.
StakeholderMap and CompetitorList components.
Week 5 â€” Forecast &amp; Notifications
ForecastPage: bar chart, heat map, leaderboard.
GET /pipeline/forecast and /pipeline/summary.
Email notifications for all deal trigger events.
CSV export endpoint.
Week 6 â€” Polish &amp; Deploy
Permission guards on all new pages and buttons.
Staleness/overdue nudge banners.
Cross-link from Lead detail page to converted Deal.
End-to-end testing (see Section 16).
Deploy and gather feedback from first users.
15.  Deployment Guide
The DMS deploys alongside the LMS with no change to the existing Vercel / Render / Supabase setup.
Run npx prisma db push on the production database to add the new tables.
Deploy the backend to Render â€” the new /deals routes are in the same Express app.
Deploy the frontend to Vercel â€” new pages are added to the existing React bundle.
Run the seed extension script: node src/prisma/seedDeals.js â€” adds the new deal permissions.
After deploy, verify: create a deal, convert a lead, move a stage, upload a Deal Room doc.
16.  Testing Checklist
Deal CRUD
Create a deal with all required fields â€” succeeds.
Attempt to create with missing title â€” 400 error with validation message.
Client Manager can create and edit their own deal.
Team Member calling POST /api/deals directly gets 403.
Conversion
Convert a QUALIFIED lead â€” deal is created, lead moves to CONVERTED.
Attempt to convert a NEW lead â€” button is absent; direct API call returns 400.
Converted lead shows &apos;View Deal&apos; link pointing to the correct deal.
Stage Machine
Move DISCOVERY â†’ PROPOSAL succeeds.
Move DISCOVERY â†’ CLOSED_WON fails (invalid transition).
Move CLOSED_WON â†’ PROPOSAL fails (terminal state).
Drag-and-drop an invalid transition on kanban â€” card snaps back with error toast.
Permissions
Team Member cannot see &apos;Convert to Deal&apos; or &apos;New Deal&apos; buttons.
Client Manager cannot see &apos;Closed Won&apos; button (requires deals.close_won).
Admin toggling deals.create off for Client Manager â€” they lose the button on re-login.
Forecast
Forecast page shows correct weighted totals (manually verify with seed data).
Adding a deal to PROPOSAL increases the Proposal total on the forecast page.
Deal Room
Upload a 5 MB PDF â€” succeeds, appears in document list.
Upload a 15 MB file â€” rejected with size error.
Upload a second version of the same doc â€” version counter increments.
